So what the former President of Parliament has said about him, about the perfection or about those elements that define the national identity has nothing to do with the bill. The national identity of this country, of this people, has been established for two and a half thousand years. It has to do with ceremonies, language, culture and religion. It does not solve these problems or mention the bill at all, because it does not seek to do so. Minister, I wouldn't change it if I were you. All I would do to remove these misunderstandings, if I were to do something, would remove the word "identity" and leave it saying "the development of Greece's national narrative in order to create a single image of the country and the institutions of the Greek state", only to erase the impression that the opposition tried to create, wrongly and completely unconsciously. Thank you very much for your tolerance.

At the same time, we are also aiming at the dismantling of the state mechanism and addressing and eliminating customer relations and reducing the size of the public sector. This is done through the practical and effective distinction between the government and the administration. ParlaMint-GR_2019-08-05-S1-commons.u399	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, today's final speech is the first and virgin to me in the Greek Parliament with a special symbolism. However, by reading its content, we find that the inventiveness of its inventors has, unfortunately, been exhausted only in the title. Hidden behind this completely false title we find the government's will to return our country back to yesterday, when customer relations were dominant and powerful. We see an agonizing effort to create a state that is cut and stitched to your party purposes. In addition to the codification work, the draft law introduces basically six key areas: Firstly, the creation of the supreme presidency of the government and the recruitment of hundreds of young officials to serve it. We are talking about a powerless and over-concentration mechanism that we all understand how it will be served, but also about the purposes it will serve. Secondly, the positive, in my view, creation of the electronic monitoring system for government work. Thirdly, the sad abolition of the Civil Service Executive Register, as well as the procedure for administering the administrations of the General Secretariats of the Ministry and the supervised bodies of Ministries, by replacing it by crisis procedures only for the post of the Administrative Secretary of the Ministry concerned. Fourthly, the arrangements to mitigate the existing provisions on the conflict of interest and the incompatibility of the Government and its servants with their activity as private persons. Fifthly, the arrangements for the creation of the so-called National Transparency Authority, which abolishes the public administration inspector, and all public control mechanisms that are not protected by such European directives are cycling under the pretext of creating only an independent authority. Sixthly, the arrangements which they transpose to the administration and the Directors-General are legal and criminal responsibility for the adoption of individual administrative acts, i.e. for all policy pursued within the Ministries, other than the legislative work itself. This bill, ladies and gentlemen, shows a certain perception of power, an antiquated concept of the functioning of institutions and democracy. In short, the power in Greece, according to Mr Mitsotakis and Mr Gerapetritis, was excessively diffuse to the ministers concerned, over-concentrated with the common mortals of the public service, but also over-controlled by competent control bodies and authorities questioning the work of the political leadership in question. These supposed errors are trying to correct the law’s authors. But, above all, the draft law takes care of the overall rupture in every sense of state's continuity. Before August, the Government repealed any procedure for the appointment of each sectoral general and special secretary and the administrations of all supervised bodies of the Ministry.
